外部DeepSeek原记录 thinking
Hmm — the box I used for the plate now captures table (center z 0.8999 vs 0.9087 earlier). Notably the plate region z became 0.8999 = table, meaning my box no longer covers the plate center; the box coordinates in the image are fixed but the eef moved, so the plate moved in the image.

This is getting messy. The plate region box [445,725]x[465,770] gave center (0.0964, 0.2078) — table-dominated.

New approach: instead of box-based tracking, compute the plate center once in world coordinates (it's static!) — the plate hasn't moved since step 0. Plate center from agentview step 0: (0.050, 0.190). From wrist step 2: (0.064, 0.185). Average (0.057, 0.188). That's my plate center. Static. 

Now the bowl: I need its center in the same world frame. The bowl region measurement is subject to clipping bias. However, notice: the bowl region measurement changed from (0.051,0.169) at step 6 to (0.0664,0.1883) at step 7 while the eef moved (+0.014,+0.014). Bowl region moved by (+0.0154, +0.0193) — consistent with the eef movement. Good, the region measure is consistent/differential.

If the true bowl center = measured + bias b (constant in the sense of measurement method, since the bowl's position in the image is now similar to before):
- Step 6: bowl_meas (0.051, 0.169), eef (0.0455, 0.2289) → offset (-0.005, 0.060)? That is bowl center y is 0.060 less than the eef y. Hmm, that seems like a lot, but plausible: the gripper clamped the far rim and the bowl hangs on the -y side? Wait, gravity hangs it below, not sideways. Actually with a parallel jaw gripper clamping a bowl rim, the bowl hangs below the clamp point, so the bowl center is roughly at the clamp xy, displaced by rim geometry: the clamp is on the rim, so bowl center = clamp point - rim radius * direction. Bowl interior bottom center ≈ (0.054, 0.187). Plate center ≈ (0.057, 0.188) [and agentview plate center (0.050,0.190)].

So the bowl IS essentially centered over the plate to within ~3mm! 

But the visual wrist image shows the plate visible on the left side of the bowl, not centered... Hmm. Explanation: the bowl's rim is high (z 1.05) and the plate is at z 0.912 — a 14cm height difference! With the wrist camera (near-vertical but slightly off-axis), the plate appears displaced laterally relative to the bowl's rim by parallax: 0.14 m of height difference × tan(tilt) — if the camera is tilted ~15°, the shift ≈ 0.037 m. That fully explains the apparent offset.

So the parallax explains the visual. The world-map measurement (bowl bottom center ≈ plate center) is trustworthy.

Note: the interior bottom center should equal the bowl's axis if the bowl is empty and the deepest visible point is central.
